            - The Huns: Distance: Close
            - No war, no denouncement listed in the summary. But in TheirOpinionToUs: "We have denounced them!" - wait, does that mean they denounced us or we denounced them? The text says "We have denounced them!" under TheirOpinionToUs, meaning they denounced us? No, "Their opinion to us" means what they think of us. So "We have denounced them!" from their perspective means they denounced us. But I also see under OurOpinionToThem: "We have denounced them!" meaning we denounced them. Wait, both have denounced each other?
            
            Actually looking at OurOpinionToThem for Denmark:
            - "We have denounced them." is NOT there.
            - "We are completely untrustworthy to them." This is about our reputation.
            - Under TheirOpinionToUs:
              - "We have denounced them!" - wait, from whose perspective? "Their opinion to us" means why they like/dislike us. So if it says "We have denounced them", it means WE denounced THEM and they know it. No, that doesn't make sense. It should say "They have denounced us" if they denounced us. But Civ diplo text is weird.
            
            Actually in Civ V diplo, the modifier text is from their perspective:
            - "We have denounced them!" means THEY denounced US.
            - "They have denounced us!" means WE denounced THEM.
            Wait, no. Let me think of it as "Their opinion of us" - the reasons listed are their reasons. So "We have denounced them!" means THEY (Denmark) have denounced US (The Huns). Because "we" refers to Denmark (the subject).
            
            But looking at Denmark's relationship summary at the bottom:
            - The Huns: Distance: Close. No diplo status listed. Unlike Netherlands which says "Denounced Them".
            
            Wait, our opinion of Indonesia says: "We have denounced them!" And indeed we have. So in "OurOpinionToThem", "We" means us.
            
            In "TheirOpinionToUs", "We" means them. So "We have denounced them!" means Denmark denounced The Huns? That would be bad.
            
            But in the bottom summary for Denmark, it doesn't show "Denounced By Them" like it does for Netherlands. So maybe the text means "We [The Huns] have denounced them [Denmark]" - which would appear under TheirOpinionToUs because it's something we did that affects their opinion.
            
            Actually I think the convention in Civ V is: the modifiers under "TheirOpinionToUs" are written from the other civ's perspective but describing actions. So "We have denounced them!" appearing under TheirOpinionToUs means "They have denounced us!" No wait... I'm overcomplicating.
            
            The point is: Denmark is a coalition partner but is fighting our other coalition partners. For the Global Hegemony vote, will Denmark vote with us? With relationship 20 and fighting our allies, maybe not.
            
            **Persona:**
            Current persona has WarBias 10, HostileBias 10, Boldness 5, AfraidBias 4. Given we're being nuked and have 1/7th the military, Boldness 5 is appropriate. WarBias 10 keeps us focused on war.
            
            **Next Tech:**
            I think after Mobile Tactics, we should go for Atomic Theory. This is essential. Let me use set-research for Atomic Theory. Wait, but what does Mobile Tactics unlock? In Vox Populi, Mobile Tactics might be required for something else. But I don't have the full tree. However, with Netherlands fielding nukes, we need our own nuclear deterrent ASAP.
            
            Actually wait - in Civ V, to build nukes you need:
            - Manhattan Project (requires Atomic Theory)
            - Nuclear Fission (for Atomic Bombs) or Fusion (for Hydrogen Bombs)
            
            Atomic Theory reveals Uranium. We need to know where Uranium is. So Atomic Theory is the gateway.
            
            **Policy:**
            Next policy in 23 turns. What should we pick?
            Given the options, if we can take Autocracy tenets:
            - Air Supremacy (Level 3): Free Airport in every city, +25% aircraft production, Zeroes. This is incredibly strong. Airports allow airlifting (critical for defense), increase air unit capacity, and the production boost helps. Given we have 6 cities, this is like 6 free multi-hundred-production buildings.
            - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1): -33% gold cost purchasing units, +3 Science from defense buildings. Since we're buying units (like the Infantry at Awasa), this saves money AND gives science.
            - Iron Fist (Level 1): Vassal stuff, not useful now.
            
            Air Supremacy seems like the best choice IF we can take a Level 3 tenet. Do we qualify? We have 6 tenets already. The prerequisites for Level 3 in VP usually require adopting the ideology + 2 level 1 tenets + 2 level 2 tenets, or something similar. We have 6 total, which should easily qualify. But we need to check if it's available. The options list it, so it must be available.
            
            However, if Air Supremacy is too expensive or we don't have the prerequisites, Military-Industrial Complex is excellent.
            
            Wait, the policy tool says we can set "Policy name or branch name to adopt next". So I can specify "Air Supremacy" or "Military-Industrial Complex".
            
            Given the imminent threat of further air attacks and the value of airports, Air Supremacy is likely the best pick. But is it more valuable than Military-Industrial Complex? With our gold income at 72 GPT, purchasing units is expensive. -33% would be huge. But free airports in 6 cities is also massive production value (each airport is ~600-800 production? And enables airlift...).
            
            Actually, looking at the game state: We're building/buying defensive military. We need troops fast. Military-Industrial Complex makes purchases cheaper. But we only have 513 gold. An Infantry costs maybe 1000+ gold. Even with discount, we can't afford many. Airports would help over time.
            
            Given we're 23 turns from next policy, and this game might end sooner (diplo vote in 6 turns), the policy choice might not matter for the immediate diplomatic victory. But it will matter for subsequent wars.
            
            Wait - if Netherlands wins Global Hegemony in 6 turns, we lose. So the next policy won't affect that. The policy choice is for later. But we still must set it.
            
            Let me think about what happens in 6 turns:
            - Global Hegemony vote
            - Netherlands has 44 delegates, needs 42
            - But they might not get all their votes if city-states don't align? No, delegates = votes.
            - Can we and our allies vote for someone else and prevent Netherlands from getting 42?
            - The rules: "If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions."
            - So to block Netherlands, we just need to make sure the highest vote-getter doesn't reach 42, OR we vote for someone else who gets more than Netherlands but less than 42? No, if someone else gets more than Netherlands but less than 42, nobody wins. That's fine. If Netherlands gets the most but less than 42, nobody wins.
            - Wait, if Netherlands has 44 delegates, they can give all 44 to themselves. Unless... delegates from city-states might not all be controlled? But the UI says Netherlands has 44 delegates. Their own votes = 44.
            - Actually in Civ V World Congress, delegates include your base delegates + city-state allies. If Netherlands has 44 total, they can allocate all 44 to themselves.
            - But the Victory Progress says VotesNeeded: 42. So if they vote 44 for themselves, they win.
            - HOW CAN WE STOP THIS?
            - We need to steal their city-state allies or reduce their delegate count below 42 before the vote.
            - OR we need a way to kill enough of their delegates.
            - The active resolution "World Ideology: Autocracy" gives them +1 delegate plus +1 for every other civ following Autocracy.
            - Civs following Autocracy: The Huns, Denmark, Mongolia, Japan, Venice? Let's check:
              - Huns: Autocracy
              - Mongolia: Order (they have Order: 5)
              - Japan: Order (Order: 5) - wait, Japan has Order 5? But OurOpinionToThem says "You have chosen to adopt the Order Ideology, but they believe in Autocracy." Again the text seems swapped or wrong. Let me check Japan's policy branches: "Order: 5". So Japan is Order, not Autocracy. Venice has Order: 4. Denmark has Autocracy: 9.
              - So Autocracy followers: Huns, Denmark. Maybe others? Indonesia has Order: 1.
              - So Netherlands gets +1 (base for World Ideology) + 1 (Denmark) + 1 (The Huns?) Wait, the description says "Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology." So the proposer (Netherlands) gets +1 + number of other Autocracy followers. If Huns and Denmark are Autocracy, Netherlands gets +1 + 2 = +3 bonus delegates from World Ideology.
              - Netherlands also gets delegates from city-states and base.
            
            If Netherlands can allocate 44 delegates to themselves and need 42, they win unless we can reduce them below 42.
            
            Can we use spies to stage coups? We have spies in Amsterdam (Netherlands), Copenhagen (Denmark), Medan (Indonesia), and Axum/Bergen (Denmark). We don't have spies in Netherlands' city-states like Quebec City, Ife, Salem, Yerevan, Bogota.
            
            Wait - we have 8 spies. Locations:
            1. Bleda: Traveling to Kathmandu
            2. Rugila: Copenhagen (Denmark) - 313 network, +23/turn
            3. Timur: Medan (Indonesia) - 337 network, +34/turn
            4. Octar: Traveling to Awasa (counterspy)
            5. Charato: Amsterdam (Netherlands) - 472 network, +11/turn
            6. Bendguz: Axum (Denmark) - 501 network, +37/turn
            7. Ernakh: Bergen (Denmark) - 500 network, +37/turn
            8. Aksungur: Attila's Court (counterspy)
            
            We have 3 spies in Denmark cities! That's interesting. We could rig elections in Denmark if they were city-states, but they're major civs. In major civs, spies steal tech, rig elections (if host had city-state allies), or stage coups (in city-states).
            
            We need to coup Netherlands' city-states to reduce their delegate count. But our spies are mostly in Denmark and Indonesia/Netherlands.
            
            Actually, looking at the events: EspionageState changes show Mongolia moved spies to Melbourne and Lisbon. Denmark moved spies to Melbourne and Lisbon. Netherlands moved spies to Lisbon and Melbourne. Everyone is spying on city-states.
            
            We have NO spies in any Netherlands-allied city-state. We need to move them. Our spies in Denmark are somewhat wasted if we're not stealing tech (Denmark has 67 techs, we have 63 - we could steal from them but we're allied). We should maybe move some to Netherlands' city-states.
            
            But I can't directly control spy movements - that's tactical. The staff members handle that based on Espionage flavor. Our Espionage flavor is 100, which is good. But the staff members might prioritize stealing tech or protecting our cities over coups.
            
            Actually, looking at our Spy Timur in Medan (Indonesia) - they have 337 network. If we rigged Medan's elections... wait, Indonesia is Order, not a city-state. Spies in major civ cities can only steal tech or identify their city-state allies. They can also increase our tourism with them (if diplomats) or disrupt their production.
            
            To reduce Netherlands' delegate count, we need to:
            1. Ally with their city-states (Quebec City, Ife, Melbourne, Salem, Yerevan, Bogota, Lisbon) - but most are at war with us, so very hard.
            2. Reduce their base delegate count (can't do much).
            3. Revoke World Ideology: Autocracy (very hard, they proposed and passed it).
            
            Wait - can the Global Hegemony vote actually be won by someone else? If all our coalition partners vote for, say, Mongolia (11 delegates), that's only 11. Netherlands gives themselves 44. Netherlands wins.
            
            UNLESS the AI splits votes or abstains based on relations. But the AI usually votes for themselves or their allies. Netherlands will definitely vote for themselves.
            
            Is there any way to prevent a civilization from voting for themselves? No.
            
            So if Netherlands truly has 44 delegates and needs 42, and can allocate all of them to themselves, they WILL win in 6 turns unless something changes.
            
            But wait - the Diplomatic Victory section says:
            - "VotesNeeded: 42"
            - "Status: World Congress - Voting in 6 turns"
            - "Proposals: Enact: Global Hegemony"
            - "Contender: The Netherlands"
            - "The Netherlands: Delegates: 44, VictoryPercentage: 104"
            
            VictoryPercentage: 104 means they exceed the needed votes by 4% (44/42 = 104%).
            
            This looks unwinnable unless we can reduce their delegates or increase the threshold. The threshold is based on number of civilizations and city-states. If more city-states are eliminated, threshold might decrease? Actually more city-states usually means higher threshold. We had some city-states defeated (Kabul, Sydney, Belgrade, etc.) but that would lower the threshold if anything, making it easier for Netherlands.
            
            Wait - has the threshold already accounted for defeated city-states? Yes, it says 42 needed currently.

            Or perhaps the coalition can unify behind a single candidate to prevent Netherlands from having the plurality? No, that's not how it works in Civ V. In Civ V diplomatic victory, you just need X delegates voting for you. It's not compared to others. If you get 42+ votes, you win. Others getting votes doesn't subtract from yours.
            
            So if Netherlands has 44 delegates and gives themselves 44 votes, they get 44 >= 42, they win. Period.
            
            The only ways to stop this:
            1. Reduce their delegate count below 42 before the vote
            2. Prevent the vote from happening (but it's automatic once World Ideology is active)
            3. Capture the United Nations? No, they built it.
            4. Capture their capital? Amsterdam is heavily defended (125 defense, wonders, 52 pop).
            5. Somehow make them lose city-state allies before the vote.

            - Quebec City (pop 25, defense 124, coastal) - ally
            - Ife (pop 15, defense 97) - ally
            - Melbourne (pop 21, at war with us) - ally
            - Salem (pop 25) - ally
            - Yerevan (pop 10) - ally
            - Bogota (pop 23) - ally
            - Lisbon (pop 3) - ally
            
            Many of these are far away (Quebec City, Ife are likely near Netherlands). Melbourne is near us. Lisbon is near us but we just lost it.
            
            Taking Melbourne or Lisbon wouldn't reduce their delegates much. And we can't peace out with them quickly (war influence -60).

No, the report says "In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state."
